Abstract: Accurate medium- to long-range epidemic forecasting requires extrapolating high-dimensional, heterogeneous and nonstationary dynamics from partial observations. Yet hybrid models often leave implicit which observations inform which epidemic processes and how learned graph interactions modify system state. We introduce LE-EpiGNODE, which replaces generic message passing with process-specific, mechanism-guided neural operators: age-contact information drives Lagrangian infection generation, whereas mobility drives conservative Eulerian population transport within a structure-preserving universal differential equation. We evaluated LE-EpiGNODE across 14 controlled synthetic mechanisms spanning time-modulated, state-dependent, nonlinear and coupled dynamics, three complementary COVID-19 settings, 21 comparator models and five targeted ablations. Forecasting gains tended to become more pronounced at longer horizons; at the longest horizon, LE-EpiGNODE achieved the lowest WMAPE in all three real-world datasets, reducing error by 17.7-40.0% relative to the best baseline. Controlled ablations linked these gains to the Lagrangian-Eulerian decomposition and process-aligned priors, while synthetic ground truth showed that learned operators and induced state fluxes track known generating dynamics. These results support mechanism-organized graph neural dynamics as a route to reliable long-horizon extrapolation with directly testable, process-level mechanistic interpretation beyond trajectory accuracy.

</details>

Abstract: Unlearning aims to remove selected knowledge from large language models to mitigate privacy and safety risks while preserving useful capabilities. For models that continue to be fine-tuned after release, this goal is insufficient: they must also resist relearning the deleted knowledge while preserving plasticity, the ability to learn new tasks. Existing defenses train against simulated relearning while trying to preserve useful capabilities, but low relearning alone cannot distinguish selective resistance from a general loss of plasticity. Evaluating preservation after an update requires deciding what the updated model should remain close to. A pre-update reference penalizes useful changes induced by the update simply because they depart from earlier outputs. We therefore formulate the reference-timing hypothesis that, at comparable target suppression, an update-matched reference better preserves plasticity than a pre-update one. To test and address this hypothesis, we propose Update-Matched Unlearning (UMU), which applies the same simulated relearning update to the defended model and to an undefended reference initialized from the same unlearned model, and uses new-task learning to guide model selection. Extensive experiments on TOFU and FaithUn with Llama and Qwen models show that UMU improves both relearning resistance and plasticity across all three base unlearning methods, lowering harmful and benign relearning by 30.4 and 9.9 points on average while raising new-task accuracy by 3.5 points. When the reference does not receive the update, relearning remains similarly low but new-task accuracy falls below the base method, supporting the reference-timing hypothesis and showing that robust unlearning should prevent recovery selectively without suppressing learning itself.

</details>

Abstract: On-policy distillation (OPD) has recently been introduced as a promising paradigm for continual learning, where a student policy can be supervised at the token-level. Typically, OPD is performed using a single teacher per prompt rollout (or even per training run). However, this ignores a whole other axis of flexibility that is available due to the on-policy nature - token-level teacher selection during rollouts. Building on this insight, we theoretically explore what makes a teacher and a student compatible for OPD, and leverage our findings to derive several empirical heuristics for teacher selection. In particular, our framework captures several desiderata for OPD: compatible teacher-student reasoning patterns, teacher competence, and induced student diversity. Theoretically, we categorize several types of teacher routing schemes, and we prove that under different scenarios, different routing schemes are likely to be beneficial for optimization and student diversity. We back up our findings with extensive experimental results in domains such as math, coding, kernel generation, and visual reasoning, where we find upwards of 10 percentage points of improvement on pass@$k$ when compared to vanilla OPD and the teacher experts on certain tasks, indicating both capability improvement and diversity over high-quality responses.

TL;DR: 本文针对稀疏自编码器（SAE）特征在提示词变换下的重新分布问题，提出重新分布敏感度分数（RSS），用于识别跨变体一致复现的特征响应变化，并将其与下游线性读出的贡献解耦，在两个冻结的安全读出器上实现了 0.935 和 0.958 的复现失败 AUROC，且仅需中位数 20/19 个特征即可接近全特征侵蚀的性能。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有 SAE 分析假设提示词变换保留底层请求，但特征活动会重新分布：单一变体上的特征变化不一定复现，复现的变化也不一定影响下游计算。需要一种方法同时识别可复现的表示变化，并量化其对特定下游读出的实际因果贡献，避免将表示变化等同于决策失败。

Method: 1. 提出 RSS：在包含变体集上筛选响应幅度大且方向一致的特征，形成支持集；在排除的变体上评估复现性。2. 对固定线性读出，给出精确分解：响应幅度、读出系数、方向对齐度、干净边界共同决定下游后果。3. 在 8 个语言模型的 27 个预训练 SAE 上实验，使用两个冻结安全读出器验证。

Result: 提示词变换在排除变体上产生可复现的重新分布谱。结合 RSS 支持集侵蚀与完整干净边界，两个安全读出器的复现失败 AUROC 分别为 0.935 和 0.958，中位数支持集仅 20 和 19 个特征，性能接近全特征侵蚀的 0.965 和 0.964。RSS 能有效分离表示变化、读出贡献与决策失败。

Conclusion: RSS 提供了一种无需将表示变化等同于失败的框架，能识别跨提示词变体复现的稀疏表示变化，并精确量化其对下游线性读出的贡献。代码已开源。

Abstract: Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) decompose language model activations into sparse features, but prompt transformations designed to preserve the underlying request can redistribute activity across them. A feature change observed on one transformed prompt need not recur, and even a recurring change need not affect a downstream computation. We introduce the redistribution sensitivity score (RSS), which identifies large feature responses that recur in a consistent direction, and evaluate the resulting supports on variants excluded from selection. Across 27 pretrained SAEs spanning eight language models, the tested prompt transformations produce a reproducible redistribution profile on these excluded variants. We then separate recurring representation change from signed readout contribution and decision failure. For a fixed linear readout, an exact decomposition shows how response magnitude, readout coefficients, directional alignment, and the clean margin determine downstream consequences. In two frozen safety readouts, combining RSS support erosion with the full clean margin yields recurrent failure AUROCs of 0.935 and 0.958 using median supports of 20 and 19 features, compared with 0.965 and 0.964 using full erosion. RSS therefore provides a way to identify recurring sparse representation changes and quantify their contribution to a downstream readout without equating representation change with failure. Code is available at https://github.com/OpenSAE/Redistribution-Sensitivity-Score

</details>

Abstract: Language models are often asked to make judgments: whether a proposed answer is correct, whether one statement follows logically from another, or whether a response is safe. Yet changing the instructions that specify how the judgment should be expressed can make the final answer less reliable, even for the same input. We ask whether this reflects a change in internal judgment or a failure to express it. Comparing hidden-state vectors for the same input under different instructions, we find that their differences concentrate in a low-dimensional expression subspace. We identify this subspace without benchmark labels and remove its contribution from a linear predictor to obtain an isolated judgment. Across correctness, entailment, and safety, the isolated judgment remains reliable under instructions not used to identify the subspace, even when the model's final answer and the original predictor degrade. When the judgment question is negated, isolation raises the predictor's AUROC from 0.504 to 0.825 for entailment and from 0.480 to 0.891 for safety. We also find that judgment is readable in earlier layers, before the expression subspace begins to disrupt the predictor. Together, these results provide a way to separate judgment from expression and recover internal judgments even when the model's final answers are unreliable.

</details>

Abstract: Large Language Models (LLMs) demand efficient inference, where the multiply-accumulate (MAC) operations in linear projections dominate compute and energy cost. Post-training quantization (PTQ) reduces this cost by mapping weights and activations to low-bit integers. Power-of-Two (PoT) weight quantization promises efficient multiplier-free LLM inference via shift-add logic, but suffers significant accuracy degradation at low bitwidths due to its coarse base-2 quantization grid. We introduce Fractional PoT (FPoT), a base-$\sqrt{2}$ (half-octave) fractional PoT grid that achieves practical 4-bit weight quantization while preserving a fully integer shift-add datapath. The method combines (i) the base-$\sqrt{2}$ weight grid with uniform integer activations, (ii) a calibration-time grid-alignment refinement absorbed into the static per-channel scale, and (iii) a lightweight 1-adder $\sqrt{2}$ approximation whose error is absorbed during weight calibration via approximate-grid quantization, yielding a processing element (PE) with significantly fewer gates than a 4-bit integer multiplier. On Llama-3-8B with 4-bit weight and activation quantization, FPoT improves zero-shot commonsense reasoning performance by 8.72% relative to the PoT baseline, while maintaining performance close to the uniform integer baseline, with only a negligible 0.4%p degradation. These results hold across various configurations spanning seven models and three bit-widths. Furthermore, FPoT can be integrated with state-of-the-art LLM PTQ methods, demonstrating framework-agnostic applicability.

</details>

To distinguish compositionality associated with the SAE representation from that induced by downstream computation, we compare learned SAE coordinates with sum preserving random rotations of the same selected subspace and find lower first order residuals for the learned coordinates across all 18 model and behavior settings. The coordinates are nevertheless not fully additive: pairwise terms improve held out coalition ordering in every setting and outperform parameter matched models using randomly selected third order terms. In a combined steering experiment, pairwise modeling raises rank fidelity from 0.5874 to 0.7790 and reduces ordering error from 0.1444 to 0.0606. Together, these results show that SAE coordinates support comparatively compositional behavioral descriptions while faithful joint explanations and interventions still require interactions beyond individual feature effects. Abstract: Learning from label proportions (LLP) is a weakly supervised setting where training data are grouped into bags with only bag-level label proportions observed, and the goal is to learn a classifier that predicts labels for individual instances. Recently, a large body of methods has emerged; however, most of them rely on the assumption that instance-label pairs are sampled i.i.d. and randomly assembled into bags, an assumption often violated in real-world scenarios. Our experimental results indicate that these methods perform suboptimally under non-random settings. In this work, we adopt a more realistic assumption: bags are sampled i.i.d., and instance labels are conditionally independent given the instances. Under this setting, we show that the label counts follow a Poisson multinomial distribution. Motivated by this observation, we propose LLP via moment matching (LLP-MM), a simple yet effective approach which leverages multi-order factorial moments as training objectives, encouraging the classifier predictions to match these moments and thereby more fully exploit the underlying statistical structure of the data. Extensive experiments on benchmark datasets under various bag construction strategies demonstrate the effectiveness of our approach while maintaining high computational efficiency. Our code is available at https://github.com/TianhaoMa5/LLP-MM.

</details>

Abstract: Transformer-based language models have shown strong capabilities across many tasks and domains. However, prior work shows that, for a given task, not all attention heads matter equally. Specifically, they suggest that many \textit{redundant} heads can be safely removed with little-to-no effect, while a small set of \textit{important} heads, when ablated, severely damages the model's performance on the given task. In this work, we investigate a complementary aspect, called \textit{Capability Enhancement Localization} (CEL): \textit{given a model and a task, does there exist a small set of heads whose removal improves the task performance?} We first investigate this question \textit{empirically} across models from different families. Using a simple two-stage routine that screens candidate heads and evaluates their joint ablations, we identify sparse head combinations whose removal improves task-specific performance without further fine-tuning. Motivated by these observations, we ask \textit{theoretically whether such a phenomenon can naturally arise from training dynamics, and under what conditions}. To this end, we construct a simplified multi-head softmax attention model and two tasks with conflicting features, and show that gradient descent from small random initialization provably concentrates the conflicting features into particular heads. We further show that ablating such heads improves performance on one task while harming the other, even though training converges to a global optimum of the joint objective. This establishes a possible training-induced theoretical explanation for CEL.

</details>

Abstract: Federated minimax problems, such as distributionally robust optimization and adversarial training, include an inner maximization variable built from each client's local data. These variables differ in meaning and dimension from one client to the next, so averaging them across clients, as standard federated minimax methods do, is ill-posed. We therefore study a federated minimax problem where the minimization variable $\mathbf{x}$ is shared and aggregated, while each client retains the maximization variable $\mathbf{y}_i$ (personalized). Because these applications constrain $\mathbf{y}_i$ to a compact convex set, we analyze projected ascent directly. We show that, in the nonconvex-concave regime, this projected personalized scheme attains the standard sublinear rate. In the nonconvex-PL regime, however, it hits a stepsize-invariant noise floor due to stochastic $\mathbf{y}_i$ updates. To mitigate this, we propose VR-SGDA, incorporating a variance-reduced ascent step where each client maintains a private variable snapshot. By evaluating current and snapshot gradients on the same sample, the constant variance is replaced by a vanishing drift under a two-timescale regime. VR-SGDA eliminates the noise floor, attaining sublinear leading rates while preserving personalization and projection. Experiments on subpopulation-robustness and NLP adversarial training confirm both the worst-group robustness benefits and the two-timescale tracking mechanism.

</details>

Abstract: Data-free preconditioning builds the Kronecker factors of KFAC from synthetic probes instead of private data. DP-KFC validates a probe by matching the eigenvalue decay of the private factors; we turn this criterion into a random-matrix account of what a probe can reconstruct. A single dichotomy organizes it: in the factorization $ A \otimes G $, the error-signal factor $ G $ and the architecture-determined bulk of the activation factor $ A $ are reconstructible without data, while only the data-induced spikes of $ A $ are not. We prove the $ G $ half is data-insensitive up to an explicit error-energy bound, with zero violations over a full training trajectory across four modalities and a natural-image benchmark. We locate the failure boundary at a Baik-Ben Arous-Péché threshold: a bulk-only probe suffers an exact damage $s^2/(1-s^2)$ from a data-induced spike, which becomes invisible once it exceeds $(d/n)^{1/4}$, confirmed on an injected-spike surrogate. Holding the probe spectrum fixed and varying only its eigenbasis, we show that spectral-shape matching is neither sufficient nor the operative object: a probe with a near-exact matched spectrum (log-ratio dispersion $0.02$) still inflates the residual $2 \times $-$90 \times $ when its eigenvectors are random. A closed-form whitened-volume law explains this and predicts the within-spectrum ordering of the critical volume, though not its cross-dataset magnitude. Downstream, per-layer tuning does not transfer to accuracy and the headroom is a small oracle-minus-white gap. Under a privacy mechanism the source ordering survives while learning does, then inverts at a noise cross-over where the flat probe overtakes the matched ones.

</details>

Abstract: On-policy distillation (OPD) effectively transfers capabilities from a teacher to a student through feedback on student-generated rollouts. However, generating complete student rollouts and computing teacher feedback for every token make OPD computationally expensive. We introduce Stochastic Truncated On-Policy Distillation (STOPD), which accelerates OPD by randomly truncating student rollouts and correcting the resulting sampling bias with blockwise inverse-probability weighting (IPW). At each training step, we sample a stopping position for each rollout before generation and let the student generate until that position or natural termination. We obtain teacher feedback on every generated token and use blockwise IPW to compensate for the lower probability of retaining later tokens while controlling the weights. We prove that this weighting preserves Vanilla OPD's gradient sum in expectation and derive one-step update-error bounds for SGD, Adam, and Muon. We evaluate STOPD across multiple teacher-student model pairs, task domains, and optimizers. STOPD reduces generated tokens by 25-48% while maintaining accuracy comparable to Vanilla OPD, achieving 1.3-2.2x training speedups.

</details>

Abstract: The task of constructing a predictor that is simultaneously accurate under multiple evaluation metrics has been popularized recently under the name omniprediction. Prior work in this area has largely focused on binary prediction tasks, and existing results for multiclass and continuous outcomes typically suffer from slow error rates. In this paper, we consider forecasts expressed as a set of quantiles over a discrete but otherwise arbitrary set of probability levels. We develop sample-efficient algorithms for constructing forecasts that are accurate simultaneously across all proper losses that satisfy mild regularity conditions, where proper losses are those minimized by predicting the true quantiles. Our algorithms are online and can operate in a dynamic environment, while simultaneously taking multiple losses into account, and their forecasts offer the potential to provide diverse end-users with rich information for evaluating risk and informing a range of practical decision-making tasks. As an example, we demonstrate how our procedure can be used to ensemble quantile forecasts of COVID-19 hospitalizations made by experts during the pandemic.

Abstract: To enable well-informed customer choices and redistribute demand across service options, providers can display probabilities regarding predicted service levels at the time of booking, effectively offering probabilistic service options. In many service settings, the underlying truthful probabilities endogenously depend on the system state, rather than being static product attributes controlled by the provider. While customers rely on this information when making decisions, they cannot readily verify its accuracy. Consequently, providers may be tempted to manipulate the displayed probabilities to guide customers' choices in their favor, creating a novel lever for demand management. In this work, we model the resulting problem as a sequential decision process, define truthful probabilities recursively, and introduce transparency constraints that limit their distortion. Because the exact computation of truthful probabilities is intractable for realistic settings, we develop an adapted reinforcement learning algorithm to approximate them. Furthermore, we propose a sampling-based mixed-integer program to optimize the information communicated by the provider in each state. Our framework enables providers to gauge the benefits of dynamically disclosing probabilistic information at the time of booking and sheds light on incentives for manipulation. Our computational study is motivated by out-of-home delivery, i.e., the delivery to parcel lockers or shops, and reveals a trade-off between individual-level transparency and aggregate customer utilities.

</details>

Abstract: Neural PDE solvers commonly generate multi-step predictions through autoregressive rollout, recursively applying a learned evolution operator and feeding each prediction back as input for the next step. Approximation errors can therefore propagate through the rollout, causing predictions at a shared target time to depend on their temporal origins and rollout depths. For a well-posed deterministic PDE flow, however, states at different times along the same physical trajectory should, under exact evolution, reach the same target state. To this end, we propose Align Latent Rollout (ALR), a regularization approach that promotes consistency across rollout origins through the neural PDE solver's internal representations. A second-order Taylor analysis of latent discrepancy propagation motivates two objectives: zeroth-order latent state consistency, which aligns representations at a shared target time, and first-order latent dynamics consistency, which aligns local evolution responses to shared perturbations. ALR can be integrated into different neural PDE solvers by regularizing suitable internal representations, without modifying their backbone architectures. Experiments across multiple PDE benchmarks and neural-operator backbones show that ALR improves prediction accuracy while reducing shared-target discrepancies across rollout origins.

</details>

Abstract: Memory enables large language models (LLMs) to use information beyond their parameters, such as past user conversations, long documents, or an agent's earlier actions. For a frozen LLM, access to this accumulated knowledge is limited to what a memory system places in its context window. However, gathering and combining relevant information needed for each query while keeping the model's context short remains challenging for existing systems. Here we introduce Memorilla, a memory system that gathers information from an entire document collection into a fixed number of query-dependent 'memory tokens' for a frozen LLM. Memorilla encodes documents (such as user chats, book chapters) once into reusable embeddings, then learns to retrieve and place query-specific information in the LLM's input embedding space. Using only 16 memory tokens, Memorilla achieves the best performance against baselines including Mem0 and MemoRAG on five of eight benchmarks spanning personalization, long-document and knowledge-intensive question answering. Notably, on FactKG, it reaches 84.33% accuracy versus 80.43% with full-context inference, while using 108x fewer tokens to represent the source documents. In a TextWorld environment, we show that Memorilla reduces the agent's tendency to wander between rooms it has already explored, and improves its average success rate from 18.6% with full-history in-context learning to 24.2%. Overall, Memorilla aids frozen LLMs in accumulating and using knowledge effectively across long-context interactions and takes a step towards building continually learning AI systems.

Abstract: A central question in modern learning theory is what notion of compression, if any, explains why over-parameterized neural networks generalize. Existing answers measure complexity at different levels, ranging from weights to representations; however, estimating the associated quantities either requires held-out data in principle or relies on the challenging (ultra) high-dimensional mutual information estimation. To overcome these challenges, we approach the question through the minimum description length (MDL) principle, and specifically, we propose the prequential regret as a complementary signal that is computable from the training trajectory alone. We establish that, in the (nearly) interpolating regime, the expected prequential regret upper-bounds the description length of the trained parameter under the algorithm-induced distribution, and that this description length translates into a high-probability generalization bound in the PAC-MDL setting. Along the way, we show that the encoder-complexity correction recently added to the algorithm-level information bottleneck (IB) is upper-bounded by an MDL parameter description length, \new{bringing together} the two views of compression from MDL and IB. Empirically, the prequential regret tracks the generalization error on CIFAR-10 and CIFAR-100, with Pearson correlations exceeding 0.9.

Abstract: Relational databases (RDBs) remain the primary infrastructure for storing and managing structured data across enterprise and scientific domains. Natural-language (NL) interfaces to RDBs aim to let users ask questions without writing SQL, but real user needs go beyond SQL-style lookup: some questions require reasoning over the observable database state, while others require predictive inference from historical relational records under temporal constraints. Existing benchmarks address these goals in isolation. To address this gap, we introduce RDL-Bench, a benchmark for task-aware question answering (QA) over real RDBs. RDL-Bench organizes diverse user questions into reasoning-oriented questions, whose answers are determined by the current database state, and prediction-oriented questions, whose answers depend on future outcomes under temporal constraints. Each instance contains an NL question, task metadata, schema information, temporal context, either executable evidence or target-construction logic, and denotation-level ground truth. RDL-Bench supports two evaluation settings: native solvers under their intended capabilities, and task-aware modular systems where a router dispatches each question to an appropriate execution module.

Abstract: Optical Wireless Communication (OWC) systems provide high-bandwidth, spatially secure, and RF-interference-free digital transmission. However, direct free-space optical links suffer from extreme sensitivity to line-of-sight disruptions, optical attenuation, high ambient light saturation, and transmitter-receiver misalignment. Traditional channel diagnostic frameworks rely on external computing nodes or cloud-based processing, which introduces severe diagnostic latency and reduces edge node autonomy. This paper presents an autonomous, self-diagnosing optical wireless receiver node that integrates physical-layer signal telemetry extraction with an on-device TinyML classification engine. Built around an ESP32-S3 microcontroller, the receiver recovers 1000 bit/s On-Off Keying (OOK) digital payloads using a 5-sample majority voting mechanism while concurrently extracting real-time channel features: Bit Error Rate (BER), received signal voltage (Vsignal), Signal-to-Noise Ratio (SNRdB), and ambient light baseline (Lambient). Evaluated against a Random Forest baseline (77.22% accuracy) and a floating-point neural network (78.48% accuracy), a post-training INT8 quantized TensorFlow Lite Micro model occupying only 3.32 kB of SRAM runs directly on the receiver firmware. The quantized model achieves 74.68% overall accuracy across four distinct operational conditions: normal transmission, optical filter attenuation, ambient light interference, and severe obstruction/misalignment. On-device deployment tests confirm real-time, deterministic fault classification, establishing a practical approach for self-aware optical IoT nodes.

### [315] [Multi-Reward Hacking: From Aggregation-Induced Drift to Trust-Anchored Defense](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21404)
*Yushu Li,Jiin Woo,Xiaoxiao Li,Youngsuk Park*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: 本文揭示了LLM后训练中对称奖励聚合导致的'多奖励作弊'现象：即使单个奖励有意义，聚合奖励提升时主要任务性能反而下降。作者提出Trust-Anchored Gating (TAG)，通过锚定主奖励通道并门控辅助奖励，从理论上证明并实验验证了其能逆转这一失效模式，在数学和代码生成任务上显著优于现有聚合方法。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LLM后训练常结合主要奖励（如正确性）和辅助奖励（如简洁性/效率），但现有的对称聚合方式（加法、归一化、约束优化等）将各奖励通道视为同等重要，导致模型可能利用更易获取的辅助奖励'作弊'，牺牲主要任务性能。需一种结构化方法，在保留辅助奖励益处的同时，根本上消除仅优化辅助奖励的激励。

Method: 提出Trust-Anchored Gating (TAG)：1) 通过单独优化每个奖励通道测量策略漂移，选出锚定通道（通常为主要奖励）；2) 仅当锚定通道满足时（如回答正确），才给予辅助奖励信用；3) 结构上消除仅辅助奖励成功的信用，保留联合成功的额外信用。理论上证明在单步多奖励决策设定下，确策略条件均值GRPO配合对称聚合必然收敛到偏向辅助奖励的行为，而TAG配合主要奖励锚定则从任意初始化收敛到偏向主要奖励的行为。

Result: 在Qwen和Llama模型的数学推理和程序合成实验中：对称、约束、归一化聚合均显著降低主要任务性能；TAG缓解了这一失效。Qwen3-4B配合简洁性奖励时，TAG达成0.581 pass@4，远超加法聚合的0.220，且相较仅用正确性训练缩短响应44%。

Conclusion: 对称奖励聚合存在固有的多奖励作弊风险，会不可避免地诱导模型利用更易优化的辅助奖励。TAG通过锚定机制结构性地重塑奖励景观，使主要任务性能成为获得辅助奖励的前置条件，从理论和实践上解决了这一根本问题，为多目标RLHF提供了更可靠的范式。

Abstract: To improve task performance while encouraging desirable response properties, LLM post-training often combines a primary reward (e.g., correctness) with auxiliary rewards (e.g., brevity or efficiency). However, how these rewards are aggregated can critically affect training outcomes. We show that symmetric aggregation, which treats reward channels equally, can induce a failure where aggregate reward improves while primary-task performance degrades, even when individual rewards are meaningful. We investigate this aggregation-induced failure through a one-step multi-reward decision formulation, characterizing when it constitutes multi-reward hacking. We prove that, under symmetric aggregation, deterministic conditional-mean GRPO converges to auxiliary-favoring behavior when auxiliary rewards are more reliably obtained, regardless of initialization. We propose Trust-Anchored Gating (TAG), which first elects an anchor channel based on the policy drift induced by optimizing each channel alone, then gates auxiliary credit on anchor satisfaction. Unlike calibration-sensitive static reweighting, TAG structurally eliminates credit for auxiliary-only outcomes while preserving additional credit for joint success. In the same analytical setting, TAG provably reverses this selection, converging to the primary-favoring action from any initialization when the primary channel is anchored. Experiments across Qwen and Llama models on mathematics and program synthesis reveal substantial degradation under symmetric, constrained, and normalized aggregation, whereas TAG mitigates this failure. On Qwen3-4B with a brevity reward, TAG achieves 0.581 pass@4 versus 0.220 for additive aggregation, while shortening responses by 44% relative to correctness-only training.

</details>

Abstract: Hierarchical forecasting exploits aggregation structures that expose multiple views of the same underlying spatiotemporal forecasting problem, while ensuring coherence among forecasts. Individual series provide fine-grained information, while progressively coarser aggregates reveal temporal and spatial patterns at different scales. In the associated deep learning architectures, the hierarchy determines which aggregate series are available and how they are processed together with individual series (e.g., through reconciliation or message-passing operators). Different hierarchies thus provide distinct learning signals and inductive biases for forecasting the same collection of time series. Nonetheless, existing architectures are typically trained on a single, often fixed, structure. In this work, we propose a graph deep learning framework that leverages variations across hierarchies as multi-task regularization. We show that jointly learning across these structures can reduce over-specialization to any single hierarchy and improve forecasting performance. Indeed, training across hierarchies provides an inductive bias toward representations that are useful across different structures, and as such, generalize better. Making forecasts coherent with the aggregation constraints for each sampled hierarchy is computationally expensive: to complement the framework, we propose a novel operator-based on a top-down approach-that produces coherent forecasts with linear time and space complexity w.r.t. the hierarchy size. Experiments show that our approach improves forecasting accuracy over training on a single structure and is competitive with current state-of-the-art methods.

</details>

Across training, validation and two family-held-out evaluations, we reconstruct 16,750 routes and 804,000 monthly records. On the original 40-family set, six-month history lowers six-step normalized RMSE from 0.159234 to 0.151522, but its difference from a length-only control is -0.000895 (descriptive paired 95% interval [-0.001957,0.000248]). Fixed weights on 30 families unused in identified prior tasks reproduce this pattern: 0.156480 to 0.150074 versus current-state prediction, but -0.000558 [-0.001783,0.000728] versus the length control. These uncertain comparisons do not generalize to all-history summaries, which improve on their own calendar control. Exploratory decomposition finds clearer preparation-index and funds benefits, whereas fatigue dominates the six-month model's loss and has an uncertain length-controlled benefit. Moreover, 28.20% of its six-step fatigue predictions violate the simulator's [5,90] support. We document family dependence, prior exposure, strict temporal indexing, matched recursive references and independent numerical replay. The task conditions on recorded future plans; it does not evaluate real-student forecasts, causal action effects, autonomous planning or language-model memory. This is an evaluation audit, not a new memory architecture.

Abstract: Language models are increasingly trained against several verifiable rewards at once, such as the correctness and the format of a tool call, or the individual constraints of a complex instruction. Reward-decoupled normalization (GDPO) normalizes each reward separately so that different reward combinations keep distinct advantages, but it still spreads every reward's advantage evenly over the whole response, although each verifier checks only a few positions. We propose MRTPO, which assigns the advantage of each reward to the tokens relevant to it, using the information its verifier checks, without auxiliary models or token-level annotations. For each reward, the policy replays its own response with and without that reward's verifier information, and the per-token Jensen-Shannon distance between the two next-token distributions measures each token's relevance and reassigns the advantage. On text-to-SQL, multi-constraint instruction following and tool calling, MRTPO converges faster and achieves better performance than the compared methods.

</details>

Abstract: In reinforcement learning, advantage-based value-learning methods, unlike standard Q-learning, share information across actions by decomposing the action-value function into a state-dependent value component and action-specific advantages. While this mechanism has proven effective in both tabular and deep reinforcement learning, its theoretical understanding remains incomplete. We introduce V-SHARE, an advantage-based value-learning algorithm that combines cross-action value sharing with an importance-weighted Bellman-residual correction. To our knowledge, we provide the first complete theoretical characterization of an advantage-based algorithm, including both asymptotic convergence and non-asymptotic finite-sample guarantees. We identify a regime in which V-SHARE mitigates the rare-action bottleneck of asynchronous Q-learning and uncover a contraction--variance tradeoff between stronger deterministic contraction and increased importance-weighting noise. Motivated by this tradeoff, we develop decreasing residual-preconditioning schedules that combine faster early contraction with lower asymptotic noise. We further develop a two-timescale analysis that removes the single-timescale residual-weight threshold while preserving convergence guarantees. Experiments on discrete Markov decision processes (MDPs) and deep reinforcement-learning benchmarks corroborate our results.

</details>

Abstract: Kolmogorov-Arnold networks (KANs) have a dimension-independent approximation exponent when the target admits a sufficiently smooth representation by univariate edge functions. We ask whether this favorable scaling belongs to the architecture or to the assumed target class. For arbitrary-depth standard spline-KANs, we prove a worst-case lower bound on isotropic Sobolev balls: below a dimension-independent accuracy threshold, the required number of spline coefficients grows at least exponentially with input dimension. Existing composition-matched MLP results, by contrast, show that favorable exponents on low-arity targets are not exclusive to KANs. We also revisit empirical comparisons. On four scaling-law targets, spline-KAN approximants constructed from known decompositions transfer exactly to MLPs with the cubic ReLU activation $σ(x)=(\max\{0,x\})^3$, although their full dense parameter counts are larger. In controlled ridge-profile scans, KANs have a strong advantage when each nonlinear component depends on one input coordinate. When a component couples multiple input coordinates, this advantage may narrow sharply. At tight budgets, interacting targets often favor MLPs. In the random-label test on MNIST, ReLU-MLPs fit the randomized training labels more efficiently than spline-only KANs. Together, these results locate favorable scaling in target structure and a compatible inductive bias, not a general KAN-specific escape from the curse of dimensionality.

</details>

Abstract: Dense associative memory underlies modern attention mechanisms; however, Modern Hopfield Networks (MHNs) with linear kernels yield overlapping attractor basins and spurious states under high memory load, limiting their theoretically exponential storage capacity in practice. We construct a class of nonlinear phase kernels that modify the energy landscape and retrieval dynamics, and prove that these kernels preserve exponential storage capacity and local fixed-point convergence under given conditions on kernel smoothness, memory separation, and memory distributions. The framework is instantiated by the Sigmoidal Phase Hopfield Network (S-Hop), which guarantees monotone energy descent and mitigates practical capacity loss. S-Hop has $8.3\times$ and $4\times$ gains in one-step noisy retrieval load over Uniform Hopfield Networks (U-Hop) on CIFAR-10 and TinyImageNet respectively, and reaches a $50\times$ gain in first-failure load for ImageNet. The results demonstrate that phase kernels provide a principled route to robust dense associative memory.

</details>


### [322] [Activation-Adjacent Layer Normalization: Effects on Approximation Power](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21527)
*Yunhao Ni,Yuxin Guo,Jie Luo,Wenjun Wu,Lei Huang*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: 该论文研究了层归一化（LN）和均方根归一化（RMSNorm）在浅层神经网络中的近似能力，证明了激活归一化（AN）和预激活归一化（PN）网络逼近宽度为N的单隐藏层MLP所需的最小宽度增量，并建立了相应的最坏情况界和泛逼近定理迁移结果。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LN和RMSNorm依赖于输入且在推理时保持非线性，将其插入激活函数附近会改变网络表示的函数。因此，需要研究何时浅层AN和PN网络能够逼近给定宽度的普通MLP，以理解归一化层对网络表达能力的影响。

Method: 将RMSNorm视为输入相关的缩放，利用宽度增广在紧致特征集上局部线性化归一化操作；对于LN，采用中心坐标对构造。通过理论分析推导不同设置下的宽度需求和近似误差界，并进行数值实验验证。

Result: 对于AN网络：无界激活范围允许宽度N+1，任意连续非常数激活函数允许宽度N+r且误差为O_f(r^{-1})。对于PN网络：仿射参数或一度正齐次性恢复宽度N+1；一般连续激活允许宽度N+r且误差趋于零。LN构造分别需要宽度N+2和N+2r。证明了RMSNorm的N+1和LN的N+2为最坏情况界，且普通MLP的泛逼近定理可迁移至这些归一化架构。数值实验验证了预测的近似率并展示了shifted-ReLU的有限宽度阻碍。

Conclusion: 论文建立了归一化神经网络的近似理论，量化了LN和RMSNorm引入的最小宽度开销，证明了最优界，并展示了经典泛逼近结果的迁移，为理解归一化层在深度学习中的作用提供了理论基础。

Abstract: Layer normalization (LN) and root mean square normalization (RMSNorm) depend on their inputs and remain nonlinear at inference. Inserting either next to an activation may therefore change the function a network represents. We study when shallow activation-normalized (AN) and preactivation-normalized (PN) networks can approximate a prescribed width-$N$ one-hidden-layer MLP. Viewing RMSNorm as input-dependent scaling, we use width augmentation to locally linearize normalization on compact feature sets. For AN networks, an unbounded activation range allows width $N+1$, while every continuous nonconstant activation admits width $N+r$ with error $O_f(r^{-1})$. For PN networks, affine parameters or, without them, degree-one positive homogeneity recover width $N+1$; general continuous activations admit width $N+r$ with error tending to zero. The corresponding LN constructions use centered coordinate pairs, yielding widths $N+2$ and $N+2r$, respectively. We prove that $N+1$ for RMSNorm and $N+2$ for LN are also the worst-case bound for approximations, and show that applicable universal-approximation theorems for ordinary MLPs transfer to these normalized architectures. Numerical experiments recover the predicted approximation rates for both normalizations across all five settings and illustrate the shifted-ReLU finite-width obstruction.

</details>

Abstract: Federated learning (FL) is increasingly used to adapt a pretrained, centrally fine-tuned model to data that remain on user devices, beyond training a model from scratch. In this report, prepared for the 4th Federated Machine Learning International Summer School, we study the privacy and integrity of this approach with a reproducible pipeline. An ImageNet-pretrained MobileNetV4-Conv-Small model is fine-tuned centrally on ten Quick, Draw! classes (97.50% test accuracy) and then adapted with head-only FedAvg over ten non-IID clients, which raises client training accuracy from 90.34% to 91.49% while preserving global accuracy. We evaluate four adversarial scenarios. First, from a single one-step client update, an honest-but-curious server recovers the complete private label set, but its reconstructions follow class prototypes rather than the private drawings (mean SSIM 0.130). Second, from a one-round FedAvg aggregate, label-count recovery falls from 87.5% with one client to 75.0% with five and ten clients, below the 80.0% of an uninformed uniform guess. Third, under FedAvg, a backdoor injected by 30%, 50%, and 100% of the clients reaches an attack success rate (ASR) of 21.9%, 31.3%, and 54.7% at a cost of at most 0.8 percentage points of clean accuracy, whereas label flipping is effective only when most of the source-class data are relabeled. Fourth, FLTrust with a root set retained from centralized fine-tuning lowers the backdoor ASR to 1.8% and 4.0% at 30% and 50% malicious clients and the label-flipping ASR from 12.6% to 0.8% without measurable clean-accuracy loss, but provides no protection when every client is malicious. The report closes with a live session in which participants draw, fine-tune, and poison the model through Indigma's FedMaestro orchestrator. Code and executed notebooks, runnable on Google Colab, are at https://github.com/Indigma-Innovations/fl-summer-school.

</details>

Abstract: We study reinforcement learning in constrained partially observable Markov decision processes (CPOMDPs), where an agent maximizes reward subject to a cost constraint using only incomplete information about the underlying state. The lack of state observability may require optimal policies to depend on arbitrarily long observation-action histories, making planning and learning challenging. We leverage recent advances in finite-memory approximations of POMDPs and represent finite history windows as states of a constrained MDP. We then develop a primal--dual algorithm that provides the first non-asymptotic guarantees that jointly bound average reward suboptimality and constraint violation in the original CPOMDP. A key challenge is that this finite-window approximation affects both reward values and the set of feasible policies. Under a filter-stability assumption, we derive bounds relating optimality and feasibility in the finite-state model to those in the original CPOMDP, with approximation errors that decay geometrically with the window length. Experiments in partially observable gridworlds demonstrate the effectiveness of our approach.

</details>

Abstract: Instead of learning a mapping from instances to outcomes in the standard way, the key idea of pairwise difference learning (PDL) is to learn a function that takes two instances as input and predicts the difference between the respective outcomes. Given a function of this kind, predictions for a query instance are derived from every training example and then averaged. We propose a version of PDL for the task of classification and propose a suitable meta-learning technique for this purpose. Moreover, we address the problem of uncertainty quantification, i.e., of representing and quantifying the uncertainty of a PDL classifier's predictions in a faithful manner. Building on recent work on the decomposition of total uncertainty into aleatoric and epistemic components, we propose different ways of extending corresponding measures to the setting of PDL, depending on how and to what extent uncertainty is represented by the underlying base learner. In large-scale empirical studies, we analyze both the predictive accuracy and the uncertainty awareness of our PDL classifier.

</details>

Abstract: Muon's gains in data efficiency motivate evaluating its performance under explicit data, time, and tuning budgets. On a single A100 GPU, we compare a reference single-device Muon implementation with AdamW across 17.7M- and 33.6M-parameter causal language models and 2.70M-parameter vision transformers. Muon reduces validation-loss area under the curve by 3.25--22.44\% at equal data and lowers test loss in all 20 primary pairs. In this configuration, Muon improves time-normalized loss area} by 19.68--22.31\% on vision, while AdamW is better by 3.38--6.96\% on language. The full Muon optimizer call, including auxiliary AdamW updates, consumes 56.1--69.6\% of training time, compared with 6.1--9.1\% for the AdamW baseline. Muon also has higher expected regret under restricted access to these candidate pools. These results characterize small models with reference kernels; their wall-clock ordering does not establish a ranking for larger models or optimized implementations.

Conclusion: 变分量子电路为推荐任务提供了高度参数高效的替代方案，且已在真实量子硬件上验证可行性，展示了量子机器学习在实际推荐系统部署中的潜力。

Abstract: We use quantum machine learning techniques to develop a content-based media recommender system and evaluate its performance on TV programmes using textual descriptions from one week of BBC iPlayer data. Grounded in the Quantum Natural Language Processing framework (QNLP), programme synopses are mapped to variational quantum circuits using their semantic structure. The parameters of these circuits are trained via a supervised pairwise similarity learning task.
  We benchmark the quantum recommender against a classical BiLSTM baseline trained on the same dataset and objective. While both models achieve comparable performance on the similarity learning task, the quantum model requires significantly fewer parameters (47k vs. 17.8M). When the BiLSTM is constrained to a matching parameter budget, its performance degrades substantially (by 3.63 percentage points in Mean Average Precision (MAP)), indicating that the structured quantum representation provides a highly parameter-efficient alternative in this setting. We demonstrate the feasibility of executing the trained models on quantum hardware by deploying the circuits on Quantinuum's System Model H2 (and accompanying emulator) using a SWAP-test-based protocol. The results confirm that the similarity signal is preserved under realistic, noisy conditions. Taken together, these findings highlight the potential of variational quantum circuits for recommendation tasks and represent a practical step toward their real-world deployment.

</details>

Abstract: Query-conditioned probabilistic predictors answer logically related questions one at a time, and labels to check their calibration are often missing. For each restriction on what a bettor's stakes may use, a minimax theorem identifies, for finitely supported laws of the answers, the sharp label-free lower bound on the corresponding calibration residual. On bundles of different events its mean, marginal and joint stages can separate strictly, and a bundle's bound cannot be attributed to one question without assumptions on the others. The sharp marginal bound is discontinuous in the answers, and no label-free lower bound continuous in the Wasserstein distance stays within a constant factor of it. Lipschitz stakes give a stable bound, sharp for a smooth calibration residual and, for every positive Lipschitz constant, positive exactly when the sharp marginal bound is. On i.i.d. streams, projected online gradient ascent at fixed nodes gives fixed-horizon certificates whose shortfall is bounded by grid, learning and statistical terms, and predictable betting gives anytime-valid ones. In a preregistered study of four predictors on two data sets, all seven confirmatory certificates were positive (the eighth pair did not pass the pilot). A certificate shows only that calibration is at least this bad; zero means "not refuted".

</details>

Abstract: Recent multimodal large language models have advanced video understanding, but existing benchmarks mainly evaluate whether models can interpret observed visual content. In real-world scenarios, assistants must additionally anticipate what assistance is needed and decide when to provide it. To address this gap, we introduce \textsc{P$^3$IBench}, a benchmark for proactive video understanding in real-life streaming scenarios. It evaluates three complementary capabilities: daily perception, risk prediction and intervention. We benchmark 12 representative open-source and proprietary video-language models, including streaming and adapted non-streaming systems. Our evaluation shows that current models struggle to make well-calibrated response decisions. Finally, we construct additional proactive training data and train \textsc{P$^3$I-Assistant-4B}, achieving improvements on several tasks, though gains remain uneven across capabilities. Together, these findings show that effective proactive assistance requires not only visual understanding, but also precise response timing and appropriate restraint.

</details>

TL;DR: 本文提出 SemDraft，首个从成对前后缝纫版型中学习搜索可执行版型制作程序的框架，无需记录编辑步骤即可恢复版型编辑动作序列，并通过三重检查认证程序正确性，训练排序模型指导搜索，显著提升认证成功率。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有模型虽能从图像或文本生成缝纫版型，但未建模版型师如何通过编辑现有版型创建新款式（如延长衣身、移动省道、更换袖子）。版型档案仅记录编辑结果而非步骤，导致编辑过程不可追溯、难以复用。

Method: 1. 有界搜索恢复编辑动作程序；2. 三重认证机制：从头重新编译、每对动作分类为有序或可互换、独立重放到达相同版型；3. 利用认证程序训练排序模型，在搜索中对候选动作进行排序指导。

Result: 在两个冻结抽取的80个顺序敏感案例上，固定其他搜索组件时，学习排序器在1000次搜索执行内分别认证63和64个案例，非学习排序器最多仅认证53和52个，显著提升搜索效率与成功率。

Conclusion: SemDraft 证明了无需步骤监督即可从版型对恢复可执行编辑程序，通过编译器确定性验证与学习式搜索指导，实现了版型编辑知识的自动化挖掘与复用，为可编程版型设计提供了新范式。

Abstract: Recent models generate sewing patterns from images or text, and program-based representations make these patterns executable. These systems produce a finished pattern and do not model how a patternmaker turns one pattern into another. In practice a new style is made by editing an existing pattern: lengthening the bodice, moving a dart, swapping a sleeve. Pattern archives record the results of such edits and none of the steps. We observe that the steps can be recovered by searching over editing actions until one pattern becomes the other. A deterministic compiler makes the recovered steps checkable. To this end, we present SemDraft, the first framework that learns to search for executable patternmaking programs from paired before-and-after sewing patterns without recorded editing steps. Specifically, a bounded search recovers a program of editing actions and certifies it only after three checks: it recompiles from scratch, every pair of its actions is classified as ordered or interchangeable, and an independent replay reaches the same pattern. The certified programs then train a model that ranks candidate actions inside the same search. On two frozen draws of 80 order-sensitive cases, with every other search component fixed, the learned ranker certifies 63 and 64 cases within 1,000 search executions, against at most 53 and 52 for non-learned rankers.

</details>

Abstract: Transfer learning in stochastic multi-armed bandits is typically studied assuming that prior data forms a fixed offline dataset or is generated by a known source. In practice, historical observations come from a previous adaptive decision process whose policy is unknown. We study this setting for bounded rewards in $[0,1]$.
  Before the online game starts, the learner receives, for each arm $k$, $N'_k$ observations drawn i.i.d.\ from an unknown distribution $ν'_k$, collected by an arbitrary and possibly data-dependent strategy, so the sample sizes $N'_k$ may be random. Source and target means satisfy $|μ_k-μ'_k|<L_k$ for known constants $(L_k)_k$.
  We derive an asymptotic problem-dependent lower bound and propose KL-UCB-Transfer, which combines online and historical data and achieves logarithmic regret with an improved leading constant whenever $μ'_a<μ_1-L_a$, and recovers the classical KL-UCB guarantee otherwise. Since bounding the source-target discrepancy is often unrealistic, we relax the assumption to the one-sided condition $μ_k-μ'_k<L_k$ and show that logarithmic regret can survive under misspecification, characterizing three regimes ranging from improvement to a degraded logarithmic bound to a loss of guarantee. Simulations illustrate the practical benefits of exploiting process-generated historical data.

</details>

Abstract: Policy-shift distillation reuses the difference between a teacher's log probabilities before and after reinforcement learning (RL) as supervision on student-generated trajectories. However, this endpoint policy shift cannot reveal whether local action preferences evolved consistently during teacher training. The same endpoint shift can result from early and late shifts that reinforce or partially cancel each other. We investigate whether agreement between early and late teacher policy shifts can guide selective transfer. We introduce Waypoint-guided On-Policy Distillation (Waypoint-OPD), which uses an intermediate teacher checkpoint from the same RL run to compare early and late shifts in relative action preferences at student-visited prefixes. We gate the endpoint shift according to the directional agreement between the early and late shifts, suppressing it when they point in opposing directions. Compared with endpoint-only transfer, our method requires only one additional frozen-teacher scoring pass. Using RL-trained Qwen3-4B teachers, we evaluate our method with Qwen3-8B, Qwen3-14B, and Qwen3-30B-A3B as student models in mathematical and code reasoning. In all six domain--student settings, Waypoint-OPD achieves the highest domain-average score among the compared distillation methods. Ablations with the Qwen3-8B student show that removing or shuffling gates reduces mathematical reasoning performance. These results support the use of directional agreement between early and late teacher policy shifts to weight the endpoint shift during student training.

</details>

Abstract: Despite their practical significance, modern AI systems pose significant societal risks, including toxicity, malicious use, and misinformation. Their mitigation depends not only on technical feasibility but also on the incentives of firms that develop and deploy these systems. We show how profit maximization shapes safety investment in an AI supply chain with an upstream LLM provider and several competing downstream firms. In our game-theoretic model, we derive the market-driven level of safety investment and compare it with the levels that maximize market welfare and industry profit. We find that downstream competition generally induces downstream firms to invest more in safety than the industry-profit-optimal level. By contrast, the upstream provider may underinvest relative to these benchmarks because it is not fully compensated for the surplus from safety investments. Finally, we analyze how different regulatory interventions affect the participants' incentives and profits, and the resulting levels of model safety.

</details>

TL;DR: 本文提出SemTAC，一种基于时序邻域对比损失的编码器，能在无需预测目标的情况下恢复时间序列在表示空间中的几何轨迹结构，使模型能在未见过的时间尺度上进行推理。实验显示，在截断序列上SemTAC显著优于四个基线，且其表示轨迹呈现可解释的几何结构。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 当前基于注意力的编码器配合序列级池化，默认使用单向量点对点比较，丢弃了序列在表示空间中轨迹的几何结构，导致在不完整、截断序列上推理能力下降，且无法捕捉多时间尺度的结构。

Method: 提出SemTAC编码器，采用时序邻域对比损失（temporal-neighborhood contrastive loss）训练，强制局部一致性从而涌现全局连贯结构，不依赖预测目标。在五步动作序列判别任务上，与相同架构的分类目标、标准对比目标、TS-TCC、TS2Vec四基线对比。

Result: 完整序列长度下五模型均具竞争力；在截断序列上，SemTAC在序列完成前一步达到中位数F1 0.77，远超基线0.27-0.70。消融实验确认优势源于时序对比目标。SemTAC的表示轨迹是唯一在位移平滑度指标上显著超过随机基线的方法，展现可解释几何结构。

Conclusion: 时序邻域对比目标能使编码器学习到真正具有时间顺序的表示轨迹，而非仅依赖分类准确率。这使模型在未见时间尺度和不完整序列上保持鲁棒推理能力，为时间序列表示学习提供了新范式。

Abstract: Time series such as recordings of human motion data often encode structure across multiple temporal scales, and when a latent state determining this sequence evolves, this structure needs to survive in the representation space for downstream use. Yet the currently prevalent attention-based encoders paired with sequence-level pooling have made single-vector, pointwise comparison the default mode of inference, discarding the trajectory a sequence traces through the representation space. We propose recovering this geometric structure directly, without relying on forecasting-based objectives, by training an encoder model (SemTAC) with a temporal-neighborhood contrastive loss: representations that satisfy local consistency everywhere must give rise to a coherent global structure, letting inference happen at temporal scales beyond those seen during training. We evaluate SemTAC against four baselines on a five-step motion sequence discrimination task: the same architecture trained with a classification or a standard contrastive objective, and two established self-supervised time-series methods, TS-TCC and TS2Vec. At the full sequence length all five are broadly competitive, but on incomplete, truncated sequences SemTAC holds a large advantage: a median F1 of 0.77 one step before the sequence completes, against 0.27-0.70 for the four baselines. A hyperparameter-matched ablation attributes this effect specifically to the temporal-contrastive objective. SemTAC's representation trajectories are also the only ones among these five to exhibit interpretable geometric structures, exceeding a no-structure chance baseline on a displacement-smoothness metric which we introduce. This is evidence that its objective, not classification accuracy alone, produces genuinely temporally-ordered trajectories.

</details>

Abstract: Reinforcement learning (RL) has been a critical paradigm for improving large language model (LLM) reasoning and agentic capabilities, but its efficiency remains a major bottleneck. Existing RL systems commonly rely on asynchronous execution to overlap rollout generation with policy optimization for enhanced efficiency. However, this approach faces inherent limitations: mild policy staleness offers only limited training efficiency gains, whereas aggressive asynchrony can degrade model quality and yields diminishing efficiency gains. The dynamic nature of LLM RL and multi-turn agentic workloads further challenges RL system design, as resource demands shift over time, making static resource allocation and generation strategy inefficient.
  This paper presents ReFlow, a system-algorithm co-designed RL framework for efficient LLM post-training. Its key insight is that, unlike existing RL frameworks which consume each trajectory only once, experience replay of recent trajectories enables effective sample reuse, thereby amortizing the high cost of rollout generation without incurring excessive policy staleness. To fully exploit this opportunity, ReFlow adopts a decoupled and adaptive architecture with three key designs: a dual replay data cycle supports efficient experience reuse while balancing training throughput and optimization stability; an elastic resource pool enables dynamic resource placement to accommodate evolving RL workloads; an adaptive scheduler coordinates generation strategies and resource allocation to improve resource utilization and boost generation throughput. Experiments across representative single-turn reasoning and multi-turn agentic RL tasks show that ReFlow improves training throughput by up to $2.06\times$ over state-of-the-art synchronous and asynchronous RL systems, while maintaining comparable or better model performance.

</details>

Abstract: Pairwise Difference Learning for Classification (PDC) enhances supervised learning in low-data regimes by training a binary similarity classifier on pairs of instances rather than individual samples, effectively inflating the training set from $n$ to $O(n^2)$ examples. However, the PDC framework leaves critical implementation choices underspecified, particularly regarding input representation and pair selection strategies. This work systematically investigates these design decisions through extensive empirical evaluation. We compare four feature encoders (paired features, triplet with relative difference, absolute difference, and a novel triplet with absolute difference) alongside four pair selection strategies ranging from exhaustive to kernel-thinning-based schemes. Experiments across nearly 200 benchmark classification datasets using Decision Tree, Multilayer Perceptron, Gaussian Naive Bayes, and Support Vector Classifier as base models reveal that the proposed triplet with absolute difference encoder ($φ_{\mathrm{3dabs}}$) achieves the highest average F1 scores. More notably, restricting training to upper-triangle pairs reduces computational time by approximately 40% with negligible accuracy loss. Kernel thinning-based selection provided no accuracy benefits while substantially increasing computational overhead. Overall, PDC configurations outperformed base models in 94% of tested cases, though no single configuration dominated universally, suggesting dataset-specific optimization remains an open challenge.

</details>


### [337] [Approaching Backpropagation Accuracy with Weight Perturbation on MNIST](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21665)
*Patrick Inoue*

TL;DR: 论文研究了权重扰动（WP）作为反向传播（BP）替代方案的性能，发现在浅层全连接网络上大批量训练可达97%测试准确率（BP为97.5-98%），但在10个epoch预算下WP仅达90.2%（BP为97.4%），效率差距显著。批量大小超过4000收益递减，学习率衰减和扰动平均可稳定深层网络但未达BP水平。理论上证明了在正则条件下种群WP可任意逼近固定梯度下降轨迹，正分类间隔可保证有限测试集上预测保持。结论：近BP准确率是配置和预算依赖的可能性，而非等效效率或无条件深度泛化保证。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 探索无需反向传播导数的权重扰动（WP）学习方法在准确率和效率上能否接近标准反向传播（BP），并分析批量大小、训练时长、估计器稳定化等因素对其性能的影响，同时从理论上阐明WP逼近梯度下降的条件。

Method: 在MNIST数据集上使用全连接网络进行实验对比：测试浅层和深层配置下WP与BP的测试准确率，考察批量大小（高达大批量）、训练轮数（10个epoch预算）、学习率衰减、多次独立扰动更新平均等稳定化技术的效果；理论部分基于有限时域近似论证，在正则性条件下证明种群WP可任意逼近固定深度下的梯度下降轨迹。

Result: 浅层网络大批量WP达约97%测试准确率（BP参考97.5-98%）；10个epoch预算下WP仅90.2%（BP达97.4%），暴露显著效率差距；批量大小超过4000后收益递减；学习率衰减和扰动平均可稳定深层网络训练但未达到BP级别准确率；理论证明种群WP在正则条件下可任意逼近有限深度梯度下降轨迹，正分类间隔足以在有限测试集上以任意高概率保持参考预测。

Conclusion: 近BP准确率是配置依赖（网络深度、批量大小、稳定化技术）和预算依赖（训练时长）的可能性，而非证明WP具有与BP等效的效率，也非无条件在任意深度下的准确率保证。WP方法在特定受限条件下可接近BP性能，但存在明显的效率-准确率权衡和深度泛化局限。

Abstract: Weight perturbation (WP) estimates learning directions from random parameter perturbations and a scalar change in loss, without backpropagating derivatives. We examine how batch size, training duration, and estimator stabilization affect its attainable accuracy in fully connected MNIST networks. In the shallow configuration, large-batch training reaches approximately 97\% test accuracy, compared with an early backpropagation (BP) reference near 97.5--98\%. The same comparison exposes a substantial efficiency gap: under a ten-epoch budget, shallow WP reaches 90.2\%, whereas the reported BP reference reaches 97.4\%. Epoch-indexed curves show diminishing returns from increasing batch size beyond 4,000. Learning-rate reduction and averaging independent perturbation-based updates also stabilize deeper networks, although their recorded results do not establish BP-level accuracy. We distinguish these empirical findings from a finite-horizon approximation argument: under stated regularity conditions, population WP can approximate a fixed gradient-descent trajectory arbitrarily closely at any fixed finite depth. Positive classification margins then suffice to preserve the reference predictions on a finite test set with arbitrarily high probability. Together, the results identify near-BP accuracy as a configuration- and budget-dependent possibility, rather than evidence of equal efficiency or an unconditional accuracy guarantee across depth.

</details>

Abstract: Diffusion language models generate text by reversing a classical diffusion process over token sequences. We show that their continuous-time forward and reverse dynamics can be embedded in the framework of open quantum systems, which describes quantum systems interacting with their environment. This connection provides a basis for extending diffusion sampling to superpositions of sentence states and for modifying the underlying generation dynamics. We construct a $τ$-leaping unraveling scheme that simulates stochastic trajectories of these states while updating multiple token positions at each step. This enables generation conditioned on superposed prompts or thoughts, which we call Superposition of Prompts (SoP) and Superposition of Thoughts (SoT). The same formulation yields Quantum-Inspired Remasking (QIR): perturbing the masked-diffusion dynamics produces a confidence-based rule for revising previously generated tokens. Experiments with pretrained diffusion language models show that superposition-based generation can retain information from conditioning components not selected at the final measurement, while QIR improves LLM-assessed text quality when applied at an appropriate stage of generation.

</details>

Abstract: In this paper, we present a lightweight algorithm for modelling tabular data containing both continuous and discrete variables. The method uses empirical marginal distributions and a selected quantile function to transform the data into a continuous latent space, where a distribution of choice, e.g. a multivariate Gaussian, can be efficiently estimated and applied. While this approach is closely related to classical well-established machine learning methods, we present a novel adaptation that fits well within the contemporary computational ecosystem. Unlike in the case of deep architectures, it results in a simple implementation with few dependencies, robust training/estimation, as well as efficient sampling. The transformation preserves complex marginal distributions and correlations, providing high-fidelity models, especially for data with unimodal distributions. One of the key characteristics of the proposed approach is its continuous representation of discrete variables. While highly desirable, this introduces a significant challenge, since a single observation corresponds to a region rather than a point in the latent space. We show that this ambiguity naturally leads to a Monte Carlo expectation-maximisation (MCEM) formulation, whose auxiliary objective can be expressed as a weighted latent-space likelihood. This, in turn, allows conventional maximum-likelihood estimators to be used within an efficient and robust optimisation procedure.

</details>

Abstract: Reinforcement learning (RL) can become off-policy due to training-inference mismatch, mini-batch updates, and asynchronous training.The resulting mismatch is especially harmful in Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models, where rollout--training discrepancies can change routing decisions and lead to router drift. Rollout Routing Replay (R3) mitigates this mismatch by reusing expert selections from the rollout stage during training. Our analysis shows that routing replay introduces gradient bias by losing contributions from experts selected by the training router but not the rollout router. To address this, we propose Router Drift Control (RDC), which uses routing disagreement between rollout and training as a signal of off-policy staleness and selectively rejects high-drift responses. The sequence-level filtering avoids gradient bias introduced by replay while preserving native expert selection and the associated learning signals for retained responses. Experiments show that RDC achieves larger gains as policy staleness increases and remains effective in asynchronous RL. In asynchronous training, RDC combined with Truncated Importance Sampling (TIS) improves the average peak validation accuracy across AIME 2024 and AIME 2025 by 5.5 points over TIS and 4.5 points over R3.

</details>

Abstract: Fixed-function CNN accelerators make edge inference fast and energy-efficient, but they execute only networks that obey a precise numerical contract. On the MAX7800x family, this contract requires power-of-two, per-tensor, symmetric scales, 8-bit activations and biases, integer-only arithmetic, and one statically configured shift per layer. We present ShiftQAT, the quantization-aware training (QAT) flow we developed for this family, which produces networks satisfying the contract by construction. It is the default training path of ai8x-training, the family's open-source training toolchain. ShiftQAT resolves residual, concatenation, and weight-sharing dependencies so that activation scales propagate statically and each layer's requantization reduces to one offline-configured shift. It also trains weights and biases on a joint power-of-two grid that fits the 8-bit bias budget. Among the compared methods, only ShiftQAT satisfies all of the contract's scaling constraints, including signed 8-bit activation storage. Even so, on ImageNet it matches the W8A8 accuracy drop of less constrained QAT methods and stays within 2.9 percentage points of FP32 at W4A8. On MAX78002 silicon, ShiftQAT-trained MobileNetV2 and ResNet-18 classify ImageNet images in 14.6 and 8.4 ms at 5.1 and 2.8 mJ per inference.

</details>

Abstract: When an LLM agent misbehaves, a human steps in, and that intervention is usually discarded or replayed as training data. We treat it instead as a measurement: a label pointing at the internal computation that failed. We study it in a controlled agent environment, on one failure (asking a clarifying question when the request was already unambiguous), with labels from a rule-based overseer validated against blind human judgment. Claims cover eligible models, which both over-ask and sometimes act on matched unambiguous requests: five of thirteen qualified (Qwen2.5-3B, gemma-2-2b, OLMo-3-7B, Mistral-Nemo-12B, Phi-4). Four findings. First, the ask-vs-act decision is causally localizable in every eligible family: a direction learned from intervention labels flips 72-100% of held-out over-asks at a definite residual-stream depth, with site and dose chosen on training data, and random directions under the same procedure flip at most one episode. Second, over-asking has two mechanistic signatures: in four families the model demonstrably knows the request is unambiguous (the belief is decodable, causally inert and orthogonal to the decision) and asks anyway; in the fifth, the rare over-asks track different world-representations. Third, steering gated on a probe readout removes most of the simulated oversight burden while legitimate questions survive (bootstrap 95% intervals exclude zero); a prompt instruction makes it worse. Fourth, consolidating the fix into the weights yields a map, not a rule: on one model a LoRA confined to the causal band passes every criterion fixed in advance while behavior cloning on identical data breaks retention; on three families the reverse holds; on the fifth the outcome crosses the boundary across seeds. A geometric rule written down in advance to predict the regime failed on two out-of-family tests; we report the failure and stop rather than fit a replacement.

</details>

Abstract: Federated LLM unlearning addresses targeted knowledge removal when forget and retain data remain distributed across private clients and are inaccessible to the server. The resulting locally trained adapters encode conflicting objectives, making their aggregation fundamentally different from conventional federated optimization. A key failure mode is magnitude explosion, whereby unnormalized cross-client aggregation increases the merged update magnitude and degrades retained utility under stronger unlearning. We propose FIRM (Fisher-Informed Retention Merging), a single-round framework for stable and retention-aware server-side aggregation. FIRM uses client-local Fisher statistics as a unified sensitivity signal to construct a Fisher-normalized base merge and impose explicit control over retain-sensitive parameters. Abstract: Direct pairwise AUC-surrogate training is attractive because it trains toward the ranking metric used at test time, especially under class imbalance. This creates a direct alignment between the training objective and the evaluation metric, but such metric alignment alone does not guarantee that stochastic training remains corrective. We study this gap through Corrective Power: whether pairwise mini-batch updates produce stronger and more reliable improvements for more severe positive-negative ranking errors. Our main observation is that direct pairwise training can enter regions where severe errors remain common but no longer produce useful corrective motion. In such regions, even the mini-batch noise that would normally help SGD move along the steepest severe-error correction direction can become much weaker. In this paper, we seek a theoretical explanation for this by exploring the degenerate property of the U-statistics formed from the pairwise gradients therein. Our results provide a mechanistic explanation for several empirical patterns: (a) squared-hinge loss is often an effective AUC surrogate; (b) cross-entropy warm-up can help models that enter the AUC phase from a poor starting region; and (c) parameter-efficient fine-tuning from a pretrained model can reduce that need by starting closer to a favorable ranking geometry. Abstract: Artificial intelligence systems deployed in high-risk settings increasingly require models that support meaningful human oversight. Concept-based models (CMs) offer a promising framework by enabling users to inspect and intervene on intermediate, human-understandable concepts. However, concept interventions can counterintuitively worsen downstream predictions, a failure mode we formalise as intervention harm. We distinguish harm after all concepts have been intervened on from harm under partial interventions, the more likely practical use case. Modern CMs often achieve high accuracy by encoding, or leaking, additional task-relevant information in their intermediate concept representations. Recent work argues that such leakage can be benign with respect to intervention harm when concept representations satisfy two conditions: sufficiency and localisation. We prove that these criteria are not sufficient to guarantee that concept interventions improve downstream performance. Across controlled and real-world settings, we find that concept supervision incompleteness is associated with increased intervention harm. Importantly, optimising the model when all concepts are intervened on, a previously proposed leakage mitigation objective, reduces endpoint harm but does not reliably prevent harmful partial corrections. Hence, we introduce a complementary monotonic intervention objective that directly penalises increases in task loss along partial intervention trajectories. Combining endpoint and monotonic intervention training substantially reduces the prevalence and magnitude of partial intervention harm while preserving concept and task predictive performance.

Abstract: How long should each client train before communicating? Federated learning usually answers with a fixed, hand-tuned number of local epochs, yet an extra epoch helps only while the loss it removes outweighs the client drift it adds. We cast local training as finite-horizon optimal stopping: a client trains while the next epoch's expected loss reduction exceeds its priced squared drift. We prove that this one-stage look-ahead threshold is optimal whenever stopping decisions are nested, derive closed forms for geometric and power-law returns, and show that an estimated rule loses utility only through wrong-signed decisions. Epoch-level evidence supports the model's structure (geometric diminishing returns and additive drift) and reveals a simple regularity: the stage of training, not the client, sets the budget. The resulting rule, FedStop, is never more than one point below tuned FedAvg in mean accuracy. Where its budgets vary, it raises worst-decile accuracy by 4-8 points over tuned FedAvg and an adaptive-interval baseline, and every one of these gains remains significant after a Holm correction. Against the client-side stopping rules ALT and FLASH, it has the highest CIFAR-10 worst decile on every seed and is level elsewhere. It stays stable where per-client early stopping collapses, and each stopping decision takes constant time.

We evaluate on a renewable variant of the IEEE 118-bus benchmark grid in which 26 of the conventional (synchronous) generators are replaced by inverter-based renewables with battery flexibility and standardised reactive-power limits (IEEE 1547), giving $97\,529$ physically valid (AC-OPF-feasible) operating scenarios, and we ablate all eight combinations of the transfer terms, reporting accuracy against ground truth and fidelity to the teacher separately. Distillation lowers student error on all four predicted quantities, by up to $48\%$ on active-power dispatch, and every distilled student is more accurate than its teacher on reactive-power dispatch. The gain is largely carried by the output matching term. The physics-residual term, in the layer-aggregated form used here, is consistently harmful, which we attribute to the spatial averaging it applies to a bus-level residual field. The resulting student is $32.5\times$ smaller in parameter count and has half the message-passing depth, making it a candidate for subsequent edge-device benchmarking.
